{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_01N7ACiAg2XwXU9cRfWCijGb","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01QWEq97cNKCA98u69SYEYAp","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/workspace/documentation.md","content":"# VS Code Diagnostic API Reference\n\n## Overview\n\nThe Diagnostic API allows VS Code extensions to report code issues (errors, warnings, information, hints) to users within the editor. Diagnostics are typically generated by language servers, linters, type checkers, or other analysis tools. They provide detailed feedback about problems in source code, including location, severity, message, and related information.\n\nThe API is built around the `vscode.languages` namespace and provides mechanisms to:\n- Create and manage diagnostic collections\n- Report diagnostics for specific files\n- Listen to changes in the diagnostic state\n- Retrieve current diagnostics across the workspace\n\n---\n\n## Core Types\n\n### DiagnosticSeverity\n\nAn enumeration representing the severity level of a diagnostic.\n\n**Signature:**\n```typescript\nexport enum DiagnosticSeverity {\n    Error = 0,\n    Warning = 1,\n    Information = 2,\n    Hint = 3\n}\n```\n\n**Values:**\n\n| Value | Number | Description |\n|-------|--------|-------------|\n| `Error` | 0 | Something not allowed by the rules of a language or other means. Errors typically prevent compilation or execution. |\n| `Warning` | 1 | Something suspicious but allowed. Warnings indicate potential issues that should be reviewed but don't necessarily prevent execution. |\n| `Information` | 2 | Something to inform about but not a problem. Used for informational messages about code patterns. |\n| `Hint` | 3 | Something to hint at a better way of doing something, like proposing a refactoring. Hints provide suggestions for improvements. |\n\n**Default Severity:**\nThe default severity when constructing a `Diagnostic` is `Error` if not specified.\n\n---\n\n### DiagnosticTag\n\nAn enumeration providing additional metadata about the type of a diagnostic for rendering purposes.\n\n**Signature:**\n```typescript\nexport enum DiagnosticTag {\n    /**\n     * Unused or unnecessary code.\n     *\n     * Diagnostics with this tag are rendered faded out. The amount of fading\n     * is controlled by the `\"editorUnnecessaryCode.opacity\"` theme color.\n     */\n    Unnecessary = 1,\n\n    /**\n     * Deprecated or obsolete code.\n     *\n     * Diagnostics with this tag are rendered with a strike through.\n     */\n    Deprecated = 2\n}\n```\n\n**Rendering:**\n- **Unnecessary**: Code appears faded. Theme color `\"editorUnnecessaryCode.opacity\"` controls opacity level. High contrast themes use `\"editorUnnecessaryCode.border\"` for underlining.\n- **Deprecated**: Code appears with a strike-through decoration.\n\n**Usage Notes:**\n- Multiple tags can be assigned to a single diagnostic via the `tags` array property.\n- Tags affect visual rendering only; they don't change the diagnostic's behavior or interaction model.\n\n---\n\n### DiagnosticRelatedInformation\n\nA class representing a related message and source code location for a diagnostic. Used to point to code locations that cause or relate to the diagnostic.\n\n**Constructor:**\n```typescript\nconstructor(location: Location, message: string)\n```\n\n**Parameters:**\n- `location: Location` - The location of the related diagnostic information. A `Location` object contains a URI and a Range.\n- `message: string` - A human-readable message describing the related information.\n\n**Properties:**\n\n| Property | Type | Description |\n|----------|------|-------------|\n| `location` | `Location` | The location of this related diagnostic information (read-only after construction). |\n| `message` | `string` | The message of this related diagnostic information (read-only after construction). |\n\n**Example Use Cases:**\n- Duplicate symbol definitions: Mark all locations where a symbol is defined in the same scope\n- Variable reference chains: Show where a variable was previously used or defined\n- Related type errors: Link type mismatch errors to their source type definition\n\n---\n\n### Diagnostic\n\nA class representing a single diagnostic, such as a compiler error or warning.\n\n**Constructor:**\n```typescript\nconstructor(range: Range, message: string, severity?: DiagnosticSeverity)\n```\n\n**Parameters:**\n- `range: Range` - The range in the file to which this diagnostic applies. Required.\n- `message: string` - The human-readable message text. Required.\n- `severity?: DiagnosticSeverity` - The severity level. Defaults to `DiagnosticSeverity.Error` if not provided.\n\n**Properties:**\n\n| Property | Type | Optional | Description |\n|----------|------|----------|-------------|\n| `range` | `Range` | No | The range to which this diagnostic applies. Diagnostics are file-scoped. |\n| `message` | `string` | No | The human-readable message describing the issue. |\n| `severity` | `DiagnosticSeverity` | No | The severity level (Error, Warning, Information, Hint). Default is Error. |\n| `source` | `string` | Yes | A human-readable string describing the source of this diagnostic, e.g., `'typescript'`, `'eslint'`, `'super lint'`. Helps users identify the tool producing the diagnostic. |\n| `code` | `string \\| number \\| {value: string \\| number, target: Uri}` | Yes | A code or identifier for this diagnostic. Used for later processing (e.g., providing code actions). Can be a simple string/number or an object with a `value` and a `target` URI for opening more information. |\n| `relatedInformation` | `DiagnosticRelatedInformation[]` | Yes | An array of related diagnostic information objects. Used to point to code locations that cause or relate to this diagnostic (e.g., all definitions when marking duplicate symbols). |\n| `tags` | `DiagnosticTag[]` | Yes | Additional metadata tags affecting visual rendering (e.g., `Unnecessary`, `Deprecated`). Influences how the diagnostic is displayed. |\n\n**Behavior Notes:**\n- Diagnostics are file-scoped and only valid within the scope of a single file. Each file has its own set of diagnostics.\n- The `range` property determines which portion of the file the diagnostic applies to. This affects where squiggles appear in the editor.\n- The `code` property should match codes you'll reference when providing code actions via `CodeActionContext`.\n- Setting `tags` affects only visual presentation; it doesn't change functional behavior.\n\n---\n\n### DiagnosticChangeEvent\n\nAn interface representing the event data fired when diagnostics change globally.\n\n**Properties:**\n\n| Property | Type | Description |\n|----------|------|-------------|\n| `uris` | `readonly Uri[]` | An array of URIs for which diagnostics have changed (either added, removed, or modified). |\n\n**Notes:**\n- This event is fired whenever diagnostics are set, deleted, or cleared via any `DiagnosticCollection`.\n- The event provides the URIs that changed, allowing consumers to react to specific file changes.\n\n---\n\n### DiagnosticCollection\n\nAn interface representing a container that manages a set of diagnostics scoped to specific resources (files).\n\n**Access:**\nCreated via `languages.createDiagnosticCollection(name?: string)`.\n\n**Properties:**\n\n| Property | Type | Description |\n|----------|------|-------------|\n| `name` | `string` (readonly) | The name of this diagnostic collection (e.g., `'typescript'`, `'eslint'`). Used by the task framework for problem matchers and displayed in the Problems panel. |\n\n**Methods:**\n\n#### `set(uri: Uri, diagnostics: readonly Diagnostic[] | undefined): void`\n\nAssign diagnostics for a specific resource. Replaces any existing diagnostics for that resource.\n\n**Parameters:**\n- `uri: Uri` - The resource URI for which to set diagnostics.\n- `diagnostics: readonly Diagnostic[] | undefined` - Array of diagnostics, or `undefined` to clear all diagnostics for this resource.\n\n**Behavior:**\n- If `diagnostics` is `undefined`, all diagnostics for the URI are removed (equivalent to `delete(uri)`).\n- Passing an empty array `[]` sets the resource to have no diagnostics.\n- Fires `onDidChangeDiagnostics` event with the changed URI.\n\n**Example:**\n```typescript\nconst diagnostic = new Diagnostic(range, 'Unused variable', DiagnosticSeverity.Warning);\ncollection.set(uri, [diagnostic]);\n\n// Clear diagnostics for a file\ncollection.set(uri, undefined);\n```\n\n---\n\n#### `set(entries: ReadonlyArray<[Uri, readonly Diagnostic[] | undefined]>): void`\n\nReplace diagnostics for multiple resources in this collection.\n\n**Parameters:**\n- `entries: ReadonlyArray<[Uri, readonly Diagnostic[] | undefined]>` - An array of tuples mapping URIs to diagnostic arrays.\n\n**Behavior:**\n- Multiple tuples with the same URI are merged. For example, `[[file1, [d1]], [file1, [d2]]]` results in both `d1` and `d2` being assigned to `file1`.\n- If a diagnostics item is `undefined`, all previous diagnostics for that URI are removed, but subsequent entries for the same URI are still processed.\n- More efficient than calling `set(uri, diagnostics)` repeatedly for bulk updates.\n- Fires `onDidChangeDiagnostics` event with all changed URIs.\n\n**Example:**\n```typescript\ncollection.set([\n    [uri1, [diagnostic1, diagnostic2]],\n    [uri2, [diagnostic3]],\n    [uri3, undefined]  // Clear all diagnostics for uri3\n]);\n```\n\n---\n\n#### `delete(uri: Uri): void`\n\nRemove all diagnostics for a specific resource.\n\n**Parameters:**\n- `uri: Uri` - The resource URI from which to remove diagnostics.\n\n**Behavior:**\n- Equivalent to calling `set(uri, undefined)`.\n- Fires `onDidChangeDiagnostics` event with the deleted URI.\n- Safe to call even if the URI has no diagnostics.\n\n---\n\n#### `clear(): void`\n\nRemove all diagnostics from this collection.\n\n**Behavior:**\n- Removes diagnostics for all resources in the collection.\n- Equivalent to calling `set(undefined)`.\n- Fires `onDidChangeDiagnostics` event with all URIs that had diagnostics.\n\n---\n\n#### `get(uri: Uri): readonly Diagnostic[] | undefined`\n\nRetrieve diagnostics for a specific resource.\n\n**Parameters:**\n- `uri: Uri` - The resource URI for which to retrieve diagnostics.\n\n**Returns:**\n- An immutable array of diagnostics for the URI, or `undefined` if no diagnostics exist.\n\n**Important:**\n- The returned array is immutable and cannot be modified. To update diagnostics, use `set()` instead.\n\n**Example:**\n```typescript\nconst diagnostics = collection.get(uri);\nif (diagnostics) {\n    for (const diag of diagnostics) {\n        console.log(diag.message);\n    }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n#### `has(uri: Uri): boolean`\n\nCheck if this collection contains diagnostics for a specific resource.\n\n**Parameters:**\n- `uri: Uri` - The resource URI to check.\n\n**Returns:**\n- `true` if the collection has diagnostics for the URI; `false` otherwise.\n\n---\n\n#### `forEach(callback: Function, thisArg?: any): void`\n\nIterate over each entry in the collection.\n\n**Parameters:**\n- `callback: (uri: Uri, diagnostics: readonly Diagnostic[], collection: DiagnosticCollection) => any` - Function called for each entry.\n- `thisArg?: any` - The `this` context to use when invoking the callback.\n\n**Behavior:**\n- Iterates over all [uri, diagnostics] pairs currently in the collection.\n- The diagnostics array is immutable within the callback.\n\n**Example:**\n```typescript\ncollection.forEach((uri, diagnostics, collection) => {\n    console.log(`${uri.fsPath}: ${diagnostics.length} diagnostics`);\n});\n```\n\n---\n\n#### `dispose(): void`\n\nDispose and free associated resources.\n\n**Behavior:**\n- Calls `clear()` internally to remove all diagnostics.\n- Fires `onDidChangeDiagnostics` event with all URIs that had diagnostics.\n- After disposal, the collection is unusable—any further method calls will throw an error.\n- Should be called when the extension is deactivated or when the collection is no longer needed.\n\n**Error Handling:**\n- Throws `Error: illegal state - object is disposed` if any method is called after disposal.\n\n**Example:**\n```typescript\ndispose() {\n    if (this.diagnosticCollection) {\n        this.diagnosticCollection.clear();\n        this.diagnosticCollection.dispose();\n    }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Functions\n\n### `createDiagnosticCollection(name?: string): DiagnosticCollection`\n\nFactory function to create a new diagnostic collection.\n\n**Parameters:**\n- `name?: string` - The name of the collection. Used in the Problems panel and by task framework for problem matchers. Common names include `'typescript'`, `'eslint'`, `'python'`. If not provided, a default name is generated.\n\n**Returns:**\n- A new `DiagnosticCollection` instance.\n\n**Behavior:**\n- Each call creates a new independent collection.\n- Multiple extensions can create collections with the same name; they are treated separately.\n- The collection name is displayed in the Problems panel to identify the source of diagnostics.\n\n**Lifecycle:**\n- The collection should be stored as a member variable so it can be disposed when the extension is deactivated.\n- Call `dispose()` during extension deactivation to free resources.\n\n**Example:**\n```typescript\nexport function activate(context: vscode.ExtensionContext) {\n    const diagnostics = vscode.languages.createDiagnosticCollection('myLinter');\n\n    // Use the collection...\n\n    context.subscriptions.push(diagnostics);  // Auto-dispose on deactivation\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n### `getDiagnostics(resource?: Uri): Diagnostic[] | [Uri, Diagnostic[]][]`\n\nRetrieve diagnostics, either for a specific resource or for the entire workspace.\n\n**Overload 1: Get diagnostics for a specific resource**\n```typescript\ngetDiagnostics(resource: Uri): Diagnostic[]\n```\n\n**Parameters:**\n- `resource: Uri` - The resource URI for which to retrieve diagnostics.\n\n**Returns:**\n- An array of all diagnostics for the resource across all collections, or an empty array if none exist.\n\n**Behavior:**\n- Aggregates diagnostics from all collections (both built-in and extension-provided).\n- Returns diagnostics in an unspecified order.\n\n**Example:**\n```typescript\nconst uri = vscode.window.activeTextEditor?.document.uri;\nif (uri) {\n    const allDiagnostics = vscode.languages.getDiagnostics(uri);\n    console.log(`${allDiagnostics.length} diagnostics in this file`);\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n**Overload 2: Get all diagnostics in the workspace**\n```typescript\ngetDiagnostics(): [Uri, Diagnostic[]][]\n```\n\n**Returns:**\n- An array of `[Uri, Diagnostic[]]` tuples representing all diagnostics in the workspace, or an empty array if none exist.\n\n**Behavior:**\n- Aggregates all diagnostics from all resources and all collections across the workspace.\n- Only includes URIs that have at least one diagnostic.\n- Returns diagnostics in an unspecified order.\n- Can be expensive for workspaces with many diagnostics.\n\n**Example:**\n```typescript\nconst allDiagnostics = vscode.languages.getDiagnostics();\nlet errorCount = 0;\nfor (const [uri, diagnostics] of allDiagnostics) {\n    errorCount += diagnostics.filter(d => d.severity === vscode.DiagnosticSeverity.Error).length;\n}\nconsole.log(`Total errors: ${errorCount}`);\n```\n\n---\n\n## Events\n\n### `onDidChangeDiagnostics: Event<DiagnosticChangeEvent>`\n\nEvent fired when diagnostics change globally (across all collections).\n\n**Event Data:**\n```typescript\ninterface DiagnosticChangeEvent {\n    readonly uris: readonly Uri[];\n}\n```\n\n**Behavior:**\n- Fires whenever diagnostics are set, deleted, or cleared via any `DiagnosticCollection`.\n- The `uris` array contains all URIs where diagnostics changed.\n- Multiple changes may be coalesced into a single event.\n\n**Subscription:**\n```typescript\nconst disposable = vscode.languages.onDidChangeDiagnostics(event => {\n    for (const uri of event.uris) {\n        console.log(`Diagnostics changed for ${uri.fsPath}`);\n    }\n});\n\ncontext.subscriptions.push(disposable);\n```\n\n**Use Cases:**\n- React to diagnostic changes in custom providers\n- Update UI elements based on diagnostic state\n- Trigger analysis or validation workflows\n- Log diagnostic changes for debugging\n\n---\n\n## Usage Examples\n\n### Basic Example: Creating and Publishing Diagnostics\n\n```typescript\nimport * as vscode from 'vscode';\n\nexport function activate(context: vscode.ExtensionContext) {\n    // Create a diagnostic collection\n    const diagnostics = vscode.languages.createDiagnosticCollection('myExtension');\n    context.subscriptions.push(diagnostics);\n\n    // Listen to file changes and validate\n    vscode.workspace.onDidChangeTextDocument(event => {\n        const doc = event.document;\n\n        // Simple validation: flag lines with TODO comments as information\n        const diags: vscode.Diagnostic[] = [];\n        for (let i = 0; i < doc.lineCount; i++) {\n            const line = doc.lineAt(i);\n            if (line.text.includes('TODO')) {\n                const range = new vscode.Range(i, 0, i, line.text.length);\n                const diagnostic = new vscode.Diagnostic(\n                    range,\n                    'TODO: Address this item',\n                    vscode.DiagnosticSeverity.Information\n                );\n                diagnostic.source = 'myExtension';\n                diags.push(diagnostic);\n            }\n        }\n\n        // Publish diagnostics\n        diagnostics.set(doc.uri, diags);\n    }, null, context.subscriptions);\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n### Advanced Example: Diagnostics with Related Information and Code Actions\n\n```typescript\nimport * as vscode from 'vscode';\n\nexport function activate(context: vscode.ExtensionContext) {\n    const diagnostics = vscode.languages.createDiagnosticCollection('duplicateChecker');\n    context.subscriptions.push(diagnostics);\n\n    // Validate file for duplicate declarations\n    vscode.workspace.onDidChangeTextDocument(event => {\n        const doc = event.document;\n        const declarations = new Map<string, vscode.Range[]>();\n\n        // Find all function declarations\n        for (let i = 0; i < doc.lineCount; i++) {\n            const line = doc.lineAt(i).text;\n            const match = line.match(/function\\s+(\\w+)\\s*\\(/);\n            if (match) {\n                const name = match[1];\n                if (!declarations.has(name)) {\n                    declarations.set(name, []);\n                }\n                declarations.get(name)!.push(doc.lineAt(i).range);\n            }\n        }\n\n        // Report duplicates\n        const diags: vscode.Diagnostic[] = [];\n        for (const [name, ranges] of declarations.entries()) {\n            if (ranges.length > 1) {\n                // Create diagnostic for each duplicate\n                for (let i = 1; i < ranges.length; i++) {\n                    const diagnostic = new vscode.Diagnostic(\n                        ranges[i],\n                        `Duplicate function declaration: '${name}' is already declared`,\n                        vscode.DiagnosticSeverity.Error\n                    );\n                    diagnostic.code = { value: 'duplicate-declaration', target: vscode.Uri.parse('https://example.com/duplicate') };\n                    diagnostic.source = 'duplicateChecker';\n\n                    // Add related information pointing to first declaration\n                    diagnostic.relatedInformation = [\n                        new vscode.DiagnosticRelatedInformation(\n                            new vscode.Location(doc.uri, ranges[0]),\n                            `First declaration of '${name}'`\n                        )\n                    ];\n\n                    diags.push(diagnostic);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        diagnostics.set(doc.uri, diags);\n    }, null, context.subscriptions);\n\n    // Register code actions for diagnostics\n    vscode.languages.registerCodeActionsProvider('*', {\n        provideCodeActions(doc, range, context) {\n            const codeActions: vscode.CodeAction[] = [];\n\n            for (const diagnostic of context.diagnostics) {\n                if (diagnostic.code?.value === 'duplicate-declaration') {\n                    const action = new vscode.CodeAction(\n                        'Remove duplicate declaration',\n                        vscode.CodeActionKind.QuickFix\n                    );\n                    action.diagnostics = [diagnostic];\n                    action.command = {\n                        title: 'Remove duplicate',\n                        command: 'editor.action.deleteLines'\n                    };\n                    codeActions.push(action);\n                }\n            }\n\n            return codeActions;\n        }\n    });\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n### Real-World Example: Git Input Validation (from VS Code source)\n\nThis example from the VS Code Git extension validates commit messages:\n\n```typescript\nimport { Diagnostic, DiagnosticCollection, DiagnosticSeverity, languages, workspace } from 'vscode';\n\nexport class GitCommitInputBoxDiagnosticsManager {\n    private readonly diagnostics: DiagnosticCollection;\n\n    constructor() {\n        this.diagnostics = languages.createDiagnosticCollection('git');\n    }\n\n    private validateCommitMessage(document: TextDocument): void {\n        const config = workspace.getConfiguration('git');\n        const inputValidation = config.get<boolean>('inputValidation', false);\n\n        if (!inputValidation) {\n            this.diagnostics.set(document.uri, undefined);\n            return;\n        }\n\n        const diags: Diagnostic[] = [];\n        const maxLength = config.get<number>('inputValidationLength', 50);\n\n        // Check for empty message\n        if (/^\\s+$/.test(document.getText())) {\n            const range = new Range(0, 0, document.lineCount - 1, 0);\n            const diag = new Diagnostic(range, 'Commit message is empty', DiagnosticSeverity.Warning);\n            diag.code = 'empty-message';\n            diags.push(diag);\n            this.diagnostics.set(document.uri, diags);\n            return;\n        }\n\n        // Check line length\n        for (let i = 0; i < document.lineCount; i++) {\n            const line = document.lineAt(i);\n            if (line.text.length > maxLength) {\n                const diag = new Diagnostic(\n                    line.range,\n                    `Line exceeds ${maxLength} characters (${line.text.length - maxLength} over)`,\n                    DiagnosticSeverity.Warning\n                );\n                diag.code = 'line-too-long';\n                diags.push(diag);\n            }\n        }\n\n        this.diagnostics.set(document.uri, diags);\n    }\n\n    public dispose(): void {\n        this.diagnostics.dispose();\n    }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Common Patterns\n\n### 1. Lifecycle Management\n\nAlways manage the collection lifecycle properly:\n\n```typescript\nexport function activate(context: vscode.ExtensionContext) {\n    const collection = vscode.languages.createDiagnosticCollection('myExtension');\n\n    // Add to context.subscriptions for automatic disposal\n    context.subscriptions.push(collection);\n\n    // ... use the collection ...\n}\n\nexport function deactivate() {\n    // Collections in context.subscriptions are automatically disposed\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Bulk Updates\n\nUse the array overload of `set()` for efficient bulk updates:\n\n```typescript\n// Inefficient: Multiple calls\nfor (const file of filesToValidate) {\n    collection.set(file, diagnostics[file]);\n}\n\n// Efficient: Single call with array\nconst entries = filesToValidate.map(file => [file, diagnostics[file]]);\ncollection.set(entries);\n```\n\n### 3. Clearing Diagnostics\n\nClear diagnostics when validation is disabled or during cleanup:\n\n```typescript\nfunction disableDiagnostics() {\n    collection.clear();  // Clear all diagnostics from this collection\n}\n\nfunction clearFile(uri: Uri) {\n    collection.set(uri, undefined);  // Clear diagnostics for specific file\n    // or: collection.delete(uri);\n}\n```\n\n### 4. Reacting to Diagnostic Changes\n\nSubscribe to global diagnostic changes:\n\n```typescript\nvscode.languages.onDidChangeDiagnostics(event => {\n    for (const uri of event.uris) {\n        // React to changes in these URIs\n        const diags = vscode.languages.getDiagnostics(uri);\n        console.log(`${uri.fsPath}: ${diags.length} diagnostics`);\n    }\n});\n```\n\n### 5. Integrating with Code Actions\n\nUse diagnostic codes to implement corresponding code actions:\n\n```typescript\n// Create diagnostic with code\nconst diagnostic = new Diagnostic(range, 'message');\ndiagnostic.code = 'my-error-code';\n\n// Later, implement code action that handles this code\nvscode.languages.registerCodeActionsProvider(selector, {\n    provideCodeActions(doc, range, context) {\n        return context.diagnostics\n            .filter(d => d.code === 'my-error-code')\n            .map(d => {\n                const action = new CodeAction('Fix issue', CodeActionKind.QuickFix);\n                action.diagnostics = [d];\n                action.command = { /* ... */ };\n                return action;\n            });\n    }\n});\n```\n\n### 6. Using Tags for Visual Categorization\n\nMark unnecessary or deprecated code:\n\n```typescript\nconst diagnostic = new Diagnostic(range, 'Unused import');\ndiagnostic.tags = [vscode.DiagnosticTag.Unnecessary];\n// Rendered faded out based on theme\n\nconst deprecatedDiag = new Diagnostic(range, 'This API is deprecated');\ndeprecatedDiag.tags = [vscode.DiagnosticTag.Deprecated];\n// Rendered with strike-through\n```\n\n---\n\n## Error Handling and Edge Cases\n\n### 1. Disposed Collection\n\nCalling methods on a disposed collection throws an error:\n\n```typescript\nconst collection = vscode.languages.createDiagnosticCollection('test');\ncollection.dispose();\n\ntry {\n    collection.set(uri, diagnostics);  // Throws Error: illegal state - object is disposed\n} catch (e) {\n    console.error('Collection is disposed');\n}\n```\n\n**Prevention:** Always check if the extension is still active before using collections. Use context.subscriptions to ensure automatic cleanup.\n\n---\n\n### 2. Missing or Invalid Range\n\nA `Diagnostic` with an invalid or out-of-bounds range may be silently ignored or cause unexpected behavior:\n\n```typescript\nconst line = doc.lineCount;  // Out of bounds!\nconst range = new Range(line, 0, line, 0);  // Invalid\nconst diagnostic = new Diagnostic(range, 'message');\ncollection.set(uri, [diagnostic]);  // May not appear in editor\n```\n\n**Best Practice:** Always validate ranges against document bounds:\n\n```typescript\nconst maxLine = Math.max(0, doc.lineCount - 1);\nconst range = new Range(Math.min(line, maxLine), 0, Math.min(line, maxLine), 0);\n```\n\n---\n\n### 3. Large Numbers of Diagnostics\n\nPublishing too many diagnostics for a single file may impact performance:\n\n```typescript\n// Potentially problematic: 1000+ diagnostics per file\nconst diagnostics = [];\nfor (let i = 0; i < 10000; i++) {\n    diagnostics.push(new Diagnostic(...));\n}\ncollection.set(uri, diagnostics);  // May cause lag\n```\n\n**Best Practice:**\n- Debounce diagnostic updates\n- Batch updates if generating many diagnostics\n- Consider filtering or suppressing lower-priority diagnostics\n\n---\n\n### 4. URI Comparison\n\nURI comparisons should account for platform differences:\n\n```typescript\n// Bad: String comparison may fail on case-insensitive filesystems\nif (uri1.toString() === uri2.toString()) { }\n\n// Good: Use URI's built-in comparison\nif (uri1.fsPath === uri2.fsPath) { }\n```\n\n---\n\n### 5. Undefined vs. Empty Array\n\nThere's a semantic difference:\n\n```typescript\ncollection.set(uri, undefined);  // Clears all diagnostics\ncollection.set(uri, []);         // Sets empty diagnostics (no issues, but tracked)\n```\n\n---\n\n## Performance Considerations\n\n1. **Debounce Validation**: Don't validate on every keystroke; debounce updates:\n\n```typescript\nlet timeout: NodeJS.Timeout;\nvscode.workspace.onDidChangeTextDocument(event => {\n    clearTimeout(timeout);\n    timeout = setTimeout(() => {\n        validateDocument(event.document);\n    }, 500);\n});\n```\n\n2. **Workspace-Wide Operations**: Getting all diagnostics can be expensive:\n\n```typescript\n// Expensive for large workspaces\nconst all = vscode.languages.getDiagnostics();\n\n// Better: Get diagnostics only when needed\nconst uri = vscode.window.activeTextEditor?.document.uri;\nif (uri) {\n    const diags = vscode.languages.getDiagnostics(uri);\n}\n```\n\n3. **Reuse Collections**: Don't create new collections frequently:\n\n```typescript\n// Bad: New collection on each activation\nexport function activate() {\n    const collection = createDiagnosticCollection();  // Every time\n}\n\n// Good: Create once, reuse\nconst collection = vscode.languages.createDiagnosticCollection('myExt');\nexport function activate() {\n    // Use existing collection\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Threading and Async Behavior\n\n- **No threading issues**: All Diagnostic API operations are synchronous and occur on the main thread.\n- **Async validation**: If your validation logic is async, store results and call `set()` when ready:\n\n```typescript\nvscode.workspace.onDidChangeTextDocument(async event => {\n    const diagnostics = await performAsyncValidation(event.document);\n    collection.set(event.document.uri, diagnostics);  // Sync API call\n});\n```\n\n---\n\n## Summary\n\nThe Diagnostic API provides a robust system for reporting code issues:\n\n| API Element | Purpose |\n|-------------|---------|\n| `Diagnostic` | Represents a single issue with range, message, and severity |\n| `DiagnosticCollection` | Manages diagnostics for multiple files |\n| `DiagnosticSeverity` | Categorizes issue importance (Error, Warning, Information, Hint) |\n| `DiagnosticTag` | Adds visual rendering hints (Unnecessary, Deprecated) |\n| `DiagnosticRelatedInformation` | Links related locations for context |\n| `createDiagnosticCollection()` | Factory for new collections |\n| `getDiagnostics()` | Retrieves current diagnostics |\n| `onDidChangeDiagnostics` | Event fired when diagnostics change |\n\nProper use of these APIs allows extensions to provide users with clear, actionable feedback about code issues directly within VS Code.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":10,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2032,"cache_read_input_tokens":55968,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":2032},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"16991e2b-f8d8-4eef-b15f-0ac967dc54ef","uuid":"3c004d96-f206-4884-981c-bd2dac39c800"}

Diagnostics are typically generated by language servers, linters, type checkers, or other analysis tools. They provide detailed feedback about problems in source code, including location, severity, message, and related information.\n\nThe API is built around the `vscode.languages` namespace and provides mechanisms to:\n- Create and manage diagnostic collections\n- Report diagnostics for specific files\n- Listen to changes in the diagnostic state\n- Retrieve current diagnostics across the workspace\n\n---\n\n## Core Types\n\n### DiagnosticSeverity\n\nAn enumeration representing the severity level of a diagnostic.\n\n**Signature:**\n```typescript\nexport enum DiagnosticSeverity {\n    Error = 0,\n    Warning = 1,\n    Information = 2,\n    Hint = 3\n}\n```\n\n**Values:**\n\n| Value | Number | Description |\n|-------|--------|-------------|\n| `Error` | 0 | Something not allowed by the rules of a language or other means. Errors typically prevent compilation or execution. |\n| `Warning` | 1 | Something suspicious but allowed. Warnings indicate potential issues that should be reviewed but don't necessarily prevent execution. |\n| `Information` | 2 | Something to inform about but not a problem. Used for informational messages about code patterns. |\n| `Hint` | 3 | Something to hint at a better way of doing something, like proposing a refactoring. Hints provide suggestions for improvements. |\n\n**Default Severity:**\nThe default severity when constructing a `Diagnostic` is `Error` if not specified.\n\n---\n\n### DiagnosticTag\n\nAn enumeration providing additional metadata about the type of a diagnostic for rendering purposes.\n\n**Signature:**\n```typescript\nexport enum DiagnosticTag {\n    /**\n     * Unused or unnecessary code.\n     *\n     * Diagnostics with this tag are rendered faded out. The amount of fading\n     * is controlled by the `\"editorUnnecessaryCode.opacity\"` theme color.\n     */\n    Unnecessary = 1,\n\n    /**\n     * Deprecated or obsolete code.\n     *\n     * Diagnostics with this tag are rendered with a strike through.\n     */\n    Deprecated = 2\n}\n```\n\n**Rendering:**\n- **Unnecessary**: Code appears faded. Theme color `\"editorUnnecessaryCode.opacity\"` controls opacity level. High contrast themes use `\"editorUnnecessaryCode.border\"` for underlining.\n- **Deprecated**: Code appears with a strike-through decoration.\n\n**Usage Notes:**\n- Multiple tags can be assigned to a single diagnostic via the `tags` array property.\n- Tags affect visual rendering only; they don't change the diagnostic's behavior or interaction model.\n\n---\n\n### DiagnosticRelatedInformation\n\nA class representing a related message and source code location for a diagnostic. Used to point to code locations that cause or relate to the diagnostic.\n\n**Constructor:**\n```typescript\nconstructor(location: Location, message: string)\n```\n\n**Parameters:**\n- `location: Location` - The location of the related diagnostic information. A `Location` object contains a URI and a Range.\n- `message: string` - A human-readable message describing the related information.\n\n**Properties:**\n\n| Property | Type | Description |\n|----------|------|-------------|\n| `location` | `Location` | The location of this related diagnostic information (read-only after construction). |\n| `message` | `string` | The message of this related diagnostic information (read-only after construction). |\n\n**Example Use Cases:**\n- Duplicate symbol definitions: Mark all locations where a symbol is defined in the same scope\n- Variable reference chains: Show where a variable was previously used or defined\n- Related type errors: Link type mismatch errors to their source type definition\n\n---\n\n### Diagnostic\n\nA class representing a single diagnostic, such as a compiler error or warning.\n\n**Constructor:**\n```typescript\nconstructor(range: Range, message: string, severity?: DiagnosticSeverity)\n```\n\n**Parameters:**\n- `range: Range` - The range in the file to which this diagnostic applies. Required.\n- `message: string` - The human-readable message text. Required.\n- `severity?: DiagnosticSeverity` - The severity level. Defaults to `DiagnosticSeverity.Error` if not provided.\n\n**Properties:**\n\n| Property | Type | Optional | Description |\n|----------|------|----------|-------------|\n| `range` | `Range` | No | The range to which this diagnostic applies. Diagnostics are file-scoped. |\n| `message` | `string` | No | The human-readable message describing the issue. |\n| `severity` | `DiagnosticSeverity` | No | The severity level (Error, Warning, Information, Hint). Default is Error. |\n| `source` | `string` | Yes | A human-readable string describing the source of this diagnostic, e.g., `'typescript'`, `'eslint'`, `'super lint'`. Helps users identify the tool producing the diagnostic. |\n| `code` | `string \\| number \\| {value: string \\| number, target: Uri}` | Yes | A code or identifier for this diagnostic. Used for later processing (e.g., providing code actions). Can be a simple string/number or an object with a `value` and a `target` URI for opening more information. |\n| `relatedInformation` | `DiagnosticRelatedInformation[]` | Yes | An array of related diagnostic information objects. Used to point to code locations that cause or relate to this diagnostic (e.g., all definitions when marking duplicate symbols). |\n| `tags` | `DiagnosticTag[]` | Yes | Additional metadata tags affecting visual rendering (e.g., `Unnecessary`, `Deprecated`). Influences how the diagnostic is displayed. |\n\n**Behavior Notes:**\n- Diagnostics are file-scoped and only valid within the scope of a single file. Each file has its own set of diagnostics.\n- The `range` property determines which portion of the file the diagnostic applies to. This affects where squiggles appear in the editor.\n- The `code` property should match codes you'll reference when providing code actions via `CodeActionContext`.\n- Setting `tags` affects only visual presentation; it doesn't change functional behavior.\n\n---\n\n### DiagnosticChangeEvent\n\nAn interface representing the event data fired when diagnostics change globally.\n\n**Properties:**\n\n| Property | Type | Description |\n|----------|------|-------------|\n| `uris` | `readonly Uri[]` | An array of URIs for which diagnostics have changed (either added, removed, or modified). |\n\n**Notes:**\n- This event is fired whenever diagnostics are set, deleted, or cleared via any `DiagnosticCollection`.\n- The event provides the URIs that changed, allowing consumers to react to specific file changes.\n\n---\n\n### DiagnosticCollection\n\nAn interface representing a container that manages a set of diagnostics scoped to specific resources (files).\n\n**Access:**\nCreated via `languages.createDiagnosticCollection(name?: string)`.\n\n**Properties:**\n\n| Property | Type | Description |\n|----------|------|-------------|\n| `name` | `string` (readonly) | The name of this diagnostic collection (e.g., `'typescript'`, `'eslint'`). Used by the task framework for problem matchers and displayed in the Problems panel. |\n\n**Methods:**\n\n#### `set(uri: Uri, diagnostics: readonly Diagnostic[] | undefined): void`\n\nAssign diagnostics for a specific resource. Replaces any existing diagnostics for that resource.\n\n**Parameters:**\n- `uri: Uri` - The resource URI for which to set diagnostics.\n- `diagnostics: readonly Diagnostic[] | undefined` - Array of diagnostics, or `undefined` to clear all diagnostics for this resource.\n\n**Behavior:**\n- If `diagnostics` is `undefined`, all diagnostics for the URI are removed (equivalent to `delete(uri)`).\n- Passing an empty array `[]` sets the resource to have no diagnostics.\n- Fires `onDidChangeDiagnostics` event with the changed URI.\n\n**Example:**\n```typescript\nconst diagnostic = new Diagnostic(range, 'Unused variable', DiagnosticSeverity.Warning);\ncollection.set(uri, [diagnostic]);\n\n// Clear diagnostics for a file\ncollection.set(uri, undefined);\n```\n\n---\n\n#### `set(entries: ReadonlyArray<[Uri, readonly Diagnostic[] | undefined]>): void`\n\nReplace diagnostics for multiple resources in this collection.\n\n**Parameters:**\n- `entries: ReadonlyArray<[Uri, readonly Diagnostic[] | undefined]>` - An array of tuples mapping URIs to diagnostic arrays.\n\n**Behavior:**\n- Multiple tuples with the same URI are merged. For example, `[[file1, [d1]], [file1, [d2]]]` results in both `d1` and `d2` being assigned to `file1`.\n- If a diagnostics item is `undefined`, all previous diagnostics for that URI are removed, but subsequent entries for the same URI are still processed.\n- More efficient than calling `set(uri, diagnostics)` repeatedly for bulk updates.\n- Fires `onDidChangeDiagnostics` event with all changed URIs.\n\n**Example:**\n```typescript\ncollection.set([\n    [uri1, [diagnostic1, diagnostic2]],\n    [uri2, [diagnostic3]],\n    [uri3, undefined]  // Clear all diagnostics for uri3\n]);\n```\n\n---\n\n#### `delete(uri: Uri): void`\n\nRemove all diagnostics for a specific resource.\n\n**Parameters:**\n- `uri: Uri` - The resource URI from which to remove diagnostics.\n\n**Behavior:**\n- Equivalent to calling `set(uri, undefined)`.\n- Fires `onDidChangeDiagnostics` event with the deleted URI.\n- Safe to call even if the URI has no diagnostics.\n\n---\n\n#### `clear(): void`\n\nRemove all diagnostics from this collection.\n\n**Behavior:**\n- Removes diagnostics for all resources in the collection.\n- Equivalent to calling `set(undefined)`.\n- Fires `onDidChangeDiagnostics` event with all URIs that had diagnostics.\n\n---\n\n#### `get(uri: Uri): readonly Diagnostic[] | undefined`\n\nRetrieve diagnostics for a specific resource.\n\n**Parameters:**\n- `uri: Uri` - The resource URI for which to retrieve diagnostics.\n\n**Returns:**\n- An immutable array of diagnostics for the URI, or `undefined` if no diagnostics exist.\n\n**Important:**\n- The returned array is immutable and cannot be modified. To update diagnostics, use `set()` instead.\n\n**Example:**\n```typescript\nconst diagnostics = collection.get(uri);\nif (diagnostics) {\n    for (const diag of diagnostics) {\n        console.log(diag.message);\n    }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n#### `has(uri: Uri): boolean`\n\nCheck if this collection contains diagnostics for a specific resource.\n\n**Parameters:**\n- `uri: Uri` - The resource URI to check.\n\n**Returns:**\n- `true` if the collection has diagnostics for the URI; `false` otherwise.\n\n---\n\n#### `forEach(callback: Function, thisArg?: any): void`\n\nIterate over each entry in the collection.\n\n**Parameters:**\n- `callback: (uri: Uri, diagnostics: readonly Diagnostic[], collection: DiagnosticCollection) => any` - Function called for each entry.\n- `thisArg?: any` - The `this` context to use when invoking the callback.\n\n**Behavior:**\n- Iterates over all [uri, diagnostics] pairs currently in the collection.\n- The diagnostics array is immutable within the callback.\n\n**Example:**\n```typescript\ncollection.forEach((uri, diagnostics, collection) => {\n    console.log(`${uri.fsPath}: ${diagnostics.length} diagnostics`);\n});\n```\n\n---\n\n#### `dispose(): void`\n\nDispose and free associated resources.\n\n**Behavior:**\n- Calls `clear()` internally to remove all diagnostics.\n- Fires `onDidChangeDiagnostics` event with all URIs that had diagnostics.\n- After disposal, the collection is unusable—any further method calls will throw an error.\n- Should be called when the extension is deactivated or when the collection is no longer needed.\n\n**Error Handling:**\n- Throws `Error: illegal state - object is disposed` if any method is called after disposal.\n\n**Example:**\n```typescript\ndispose() {\n    if (this.diagnosticCollection) {\n        this.diagnosticCollection.clear();\n        this.diagnosticCollection.dispose();\n    }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Functions\n\n### `createDiagnosticCollection(name?: string): DiagnosticCollection`\n\nFactory function to create a new diagnostic collection.\n\n**Parameters:**\n- `name?: string` - The name of the collection. Used in the Problems panel and by task framework for problem matchers. Common names include `'typescript'`, `'eslint'`, `'python'`. If not provided, a default name is generated.\n\n**Returns:**\n- A new `DiagnosticCollection` instance.\n\n**Behavior:**\n- Each call creates a new independent collection.\n- Multiple extensions can create collections with the same name; they are treated separately.\n- The collection name is displayed in the Problems panel to identify the source of diagnostics.\n\n**Lifecycle:**\n- The collection should be stored as a member variable so it can be disposed when the extension is deactivated.\n- Call `dispose()` during extension deactivation to free resources.\n\n**Example:**\n```typescript\nexport function activate(context: vscode.ExtensionContext) {\n    const diagnostics = vscode.languages.createDiagnosticCollection('myLinter');\n\n    // Use the collection...\n\n    context.subscriptions.push(diagnostics);  // Auto-dispose on deactivation\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n### `getDiagnostics(resource?: Uri): Diagnostic[] | [Uri, Diagnostic[]][]`\n\nRetrieve diagnostics, either for a specific resource or for the entire workspace.\n\n**Overload 1: Get diagnostics for a specific resource**\n```typescript\ngetDiagnostics(resource: Uri): Diagnostic[]\n```\n\n**Parameters:**\n- `resource: Uri` - The resource URI for which to retrieve diagnostics.\n\n**Returns:**\n- An array of all diagnostics for the resource across all collections, or an empty array if none exist.\n\n**Behavior:**\n- Aggregates diagnostics from all collections (both built-in and extension-provided).\n- Returns diagnostics in an unspecified order.\n\n**Example:**\n```typescript\nconst uri = vscode.window.activeTextEditor?.document.uri;\nif (uri) {\n    const allDiagnostics = vscode.languages.getDiagnostics(uri);\n    console.log(`${allDiagnostics.length} diagnostics in this file`);\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n**Overload 2: Get all diagnostics in the workspace**\n```typescript\ngetDiagnostics(): [Uri, Diagnostic[]][]\n```\n\n**Returns:**\n- An array of `[Uri, Diagnostic[]]` tuples representing all diagnostics in the workspace, or an empty array if none exist.\n\n**Behavior:**\n- Aggregates all diagnostics from all resources and all collections across the workspace.\n- Only includes URIs that have at least one diagnostic.\n- Returns diagnostics in an unspecified order.\n- Can be expensive for workspaces with many diagnostics.\n\n**Example:**\n```typescript\nconst allDiagnostics = vscode.languages.getDiagnostics();\nlet errorCount = 0;\nfor (const [uri, diagnostics] of allDiagnostics) {\n    errorCount += diagnostics.filter(d => d.severity === vscode.DiagnosticSeverity.Error).length;\n}\nconsole.log(`Total errors: ${errorCount}`);\n```\n\n---\n\n## Events\n\n### `onDidChangeDiagnostics: Event<DiagnosticChangeEvent>`\n\nEvent fired when diagnostics change globally (across all collections).\n\n**Event Data:**\n```typescript\ninterface DiagnosticChangeEvent {\n    readonly uris: readonly Uri[];\n}\n```\n\n**Behavior:**\n- Fires whenever diagnostics are set, deleted, or cleared via any `DiagnosticCollection`.\n- The `uris` array contains all URIs where diagnostics changed.\n- Multiple changes may be coalesced into a single event.\n\n**Subscription:**\n```typescript\nconst disposable = vscode.languages.onDidChangeDiagnostics(event => {\n    for (const uri of event.uris) {\n        console.log(`Diagnostics changed for ${uri.fsPath}`);\n    }\n});\n\ncontext.subscriptions.push(disposable);\n```\n\n**Use Cases:**\n- React to diagnostic changes in custom providers\n- Update UI elements based on diagnostic state\n- Trigger analysis or validation workflows\n- Log diagnostic changes for debugging\n\n---\n\n## Usage Examples\n\n### Basic Example: Creating and Publishing Diagnostics\n\n```typescript\nimport * as vscode from 'vscode';\n\nexport function activate(context: vscode.ExtensionContext) {\n    // Create a diagnostic collection\n    const diagnostics = vscode.languages.createDiagnosticCollection('myExtension');\n    context.subscriptions.push(diagnostics);\n\n    // Listen to file changes and validate\n    vscode.workspace.onDidChangeTextDocument(event => {\n        const doc = event.document;\n\n        // Simple validation: flag lines with TODO comments as information\n        const diags: vscode.Diagnostic[] = [];\n        for (let i = 0; i < doc.lineCount; i++) {\n            const line = doc.lineAt(i);\n            if (line.text.includes('TODO')) {\n                const range = new vscode.Range(i, 0, i, line.text.length);\n                const diagnostic = new vscode.Diagnostic(\n                    range,\n                    'TODO: Address this item',\n                    vscode.DiagnosticSeverity.Information\n                );\n                diagnostic.source = 'myExtension';\n                diags.push(diagnostic);\n            }\n        }\n\n        // Publish diagnostics\n        diagnostics.set(doc.uri, diags);\n    }, null, context.subscriptions);\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n### Advanced Example: Diagnostics with Related Information and Code Actions\n\n```typescript\nimport * as vscode from 'vscode';\n\nexport function activate(context: vscode.ExtensionContext) {\n    const diagnostics = vscode.languages.createDiagnosticCollection('duplicateChecker');\n    context.subscriptions.push(diagnostics);\n\n    // Validate file for duplicate declarations\n    vscode.workspace.onDidChangeTextDocument(event => {\n        const doc = event.document;\n        const declarations = new Map<string, vscode.Range[]>();\n\n        // Find all function declarations\n        for (let i = 0; i < doc.lineCount; i++) {\n            const line = doc.lineAt(i).text;\n            const match = line.match(/function\\s+(\\w+)\\s*\\(/);\n            if (match) {\n                const name = match[1];\n                if (!declarations.has(name)) {\n                    declarations.set(name, []);\n                }\n                declarations.get(name)!.push(doc.lineAt(i).range);\n            }\n        }\n\n        // Report duplicates\n        const diags: vscode.Diagnostic[] = [];\n        for (const [name, ranges] of declarations.entries()) {\n            if (ranges.length > 1) {\n                // Create diagnostic for each duplicate\n                for (let i = 1; i < ranges.length; i++) {\n                    const diagnostic = new vscode.Diagnostic(\n                        ranges[i],\n                        `Duplicate function declaration: '${name}' is already declared`,\n                        vscode.DiagnosticSeverity.Error\n                    );\n                    diagnostic.code = { value: 'duplicate-declaration', target: vscode.Uri.parse('https://example.com/duplicate') };\n                    diagnostic.source = 'duplicateChecker';\n\n                    // Add related information pointing to first declaration\n                    diagnostic.relatedInformation = [\n                        new vscode.DiagnosticRelatedInformation(\n                            new vscode.Location(doc.uri, ranges[0]),\n                            `First declaration of '${name}'`\n                        )\n                    ];\n\n                    diags.push(diagnostic);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        diagnostics.set(doc.uri, diags);\n    }, null, context.subscriptions);\n\n    // Register code actions for diagnostics\n    vscode.languages.registerCodeActionsProvider('*', {\n        provideCodeActions(doc, range, context) {\n            const codeActions: vscode.CodeAction[] = [];\n\n            for (const diagnostic of context.diagnostics) {\n                if (diagnostic.code?.value === 'duplicate-declaration') {\n                    const action = new vscode.CodeAction(\n                        'Remove duplicate declaration',\n                        vscode.CodeActionKind.QuickFix\n                    );\n                    action.diagnostics = [diagnostic];\n                    action.command = {\n                        title: 'Remove duplicate',\n                        command: 'editor.action.deleteLines'\n                    };\n                    codeActions.push(action);\n                }\n            }\n\n            return codeActions;\n        }\n    });\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n### Real-World Example: Git Input Validation (from VS Code source)\n\nThis example from the VS Code Git extension validates commit messages:\n\n```typescript\nimport { Diagnostic, DiagnosticCollection, DiagnosticSeverity, languages, workspace } from 'vscode';\n\nexport class GitCommitInputBoxDiagnosticsManager {\n    private readonly diagnostics: DiagnosticCollection;\n\n    constructor() {\n        this.diagnostics = languages.createDiagnosticCollection('git');\n    }\n\n    private validateCommitMessage(document: TextDocument): void {\n        const config = workspace.getConfiguration('git');\n        const inputValidation = config.get<boolean>('inputValidation', false);\n\n        if (!inputValidation) {\n            this.diagnostics.set(document.uri, undefined);\n            return;\n        }\n\n        const diags: Diagnostic[] = [];\n        const maxLength = config.get<number>('inputValidationLength', 50);\n\n        // Check for empty message\n        if (/^\\s+$/.test(document.getText())) {\n            const range = new Range(0, 0, document.lineCount - 1, 0);\n            const diag = new Diagnostic(range, 'Commit message is empty', DiagnosticSeverity.Warning);\n            diag.code = 'empty-message';\n            diags.push(diag);\n            this.diagnostics.set(document.uri, diags);\n            return;\n        }\n\n        // Check line length\n        for (let i = 0; i < document.lineCount; i++) {\n            const line = document.lineAt(i);\n            if (line.text.length > maxLength) {\n                const diag = new Diagnostic(\n                    line.range,\n                    `Line exceeds ${maxLength} characters (${line.text.length - maxLength} over)`,\n                    DiagnosticSeverity.Warning\n                );\n                diag.code = 'line-too-long';\n                diags.push(diag);\n            }\n        }\n\n        this.diagnostics.set(document.uri, diags);\n    }\n\n    public dispose(): void {\n        this.diagnostics.dispose();\n    }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Common Patterns\n\n### 1. Lifecycle Management\n\nAlways manage the collection lifecycle properly:\n\n```typescript\nexport function activate(context: vscode.ExtensionContext) {\n    const collection = vscode.languages.createDiagnosticCollection('myExtension');\n\n    // Add to context.subscriptions for automatic disposal\n    context.subscriptions.push(collection);\n\n    // ... use the collection ...\n}\n\nexport function deactivate() {\n    // Collections in context.subscriptions are automatically disposed\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Bulk Updates\n\nUse the array overload of `set()` for efficient bulk updates:\n\n```typescript\n// Inefficient: Multiple calls\nfor (const file of filesToValidate) {\n    collection.set(file, diagnostics[file]);\n}\n\n// Efficient: Single call with array\nconst entries = filesToValidate.map(file => [file, diagnostics[file]]);\ncollection.set(entries);\n```\n\n### 3. Clearing Diagnostics\n\nClear diagnostics when validation is disabled or during cleanup:\n\n```typescript\nfunction disableDiagnostics() {\n    collection.clear();  // Clear all diagnostics from this collection\n}\n\nfunction clearFile(uri: Uri) {\n    collection.set(uri, undefined);  // Clear diagnostics for specific file\n    // or: collection.delete(uri);\n}\n```\n\n### 4. Reacting to Diagnostic Changes\n\nSubscribe to global diagnostic changes:\n\n```typescript\nvscode.languages.onDidChangeDiagnostics(event => {\n    for (const uri of event.uris) {\n        // React to changes in these URIs\n        const diags = vscode.languages.getDiagnostics(uri);\n        console.log(`${uri.fsPath}: ${diags.length} diagnostics`);\n    }\n});\n```\n\n### 5. Integrating with Code Actions\n\nUse diagnostic codes to implement corresponding code actions:\n\n```typescript\n// Create diagnostic with code\nconst diagnostic = new Diagnostic(range, 'message');\ndiagnostic.code = 'my-error-code';\n\n// Later, implement code action that handles this code\nvscode.languages.registerCodeActionsProvider(selector, {\n    provideCodeActions(doc, range, context) {\n        return context.diagnostics\n            .filter(d => d.code === 'my-error-code')\n            .map(d => {\n                const action = new CodeAction('Fix issue', CodeActionKind.QuickFix);\n                action.diagnostics = [d];\n                action.command = { /* ... */ };\n                return action;\n            });\n    }\n});\n```\n\n### 6. Using Tags for Visual Categorization\n\nMark unnecessary or deprecated code:\n\n```typescript\nconst diagnostic = new Diagnostic(range, 'Unused import');\ndiagnostic.tags = [vscode.DiagnosticTag.Unnecessary];\n// Rendered faded out based on theme\n\nconst deprecatedDiag = new Diagnostic(range, 'This API is deprecated');\ndeprecatedDiag.tags = [vscode.DiagnosticTag.Deprecated];\n// Rendered with strike-through\n```\n\n---\n\n## Error Handling and Edge Cases\n\n### 1. Disposed Collection\n\nCalling methods on a disposed collection throws an error:\n\n```typescript\nconst collection = vscode.languages.createDiagnosticCollection('test');\ncollection.dispose();\n\ntry {\n    collection.set(uri, diagnostics);  // Throws Error: illegal state - object is disposed\n} catch (e) {\n    console.error('Collection is disposed');\n}\n```\n\n**Prevention:** Always check if the extension is still active before using collections. Use context.subscriptions to ensure automatic cleanup.\n\n---\n\n### 2. Missing or Invalid Range\n\nA `Diagnostic` with an invalid or out-of-bounds range may be silently ignored or cause unexpected behavior:\n\n```typescript\nconst line = doc.lineCount;  // Out of bounds!\nconst range = new Range(line, 0, line, 0);  // Invalid\nconst diagnostic = new Diagnostic(range, 'message');\ncollection.set(uri, [diagnostic]);  // May not appear in editor\n```\n\n**Best Practice:** Always validate ranges against document bounds:\n\n```typescript\nconst maxLine = Math.max(0, doc.lineCount - 1);\nconst range = new Range(Math.min(line, maxLine), 0, Math.min(line, maxLine), 0);\n```\n\n---\n\n### 3. Large Numbers of Diagnostics\n\nPublishing too many diagnostics for a single file may impact performance:\n\n```typescript\n// Potentially problematic: 1000+ diagnostics per file\nconst diagnostics = [];\nfor (let i = 0; i < 10000; i++) {\n    diagnostics.push(new Diagnostic(...));\n}\ncollection.set(uri, diagnostics);  // May cause lag\n```\n\n**Best Practice:**\n- Debounce diagnostic updates\n- Batch updates if generating many diagnostics\n- Consider filtering or suppressing lower-priority diagnostics\n\n---\n\n### 4. URI Comparison\n\nURI comparisons should account for platform differences:\n\n```typescript\n// Bad: String comparison may fail on case-insensitive filesystems\nif (uri1.toString() === uri2.toString()) { }\n\n// Good: Use URI's built-in comparison\nif (uri1.fsPath === uri2.fsPath) { }\n```\n\n---\n\n### 5. Undefined vs. Empty Array\n\nThere's a semantic difference:\n\n```typescript\ncollection.set(uri, undefined);  // Clears all diagnostics\ncollection.set(uri, []);         // Sets empty diagnostics (no issues, but tracked)\n```\n\n---\n\n## Performance Considerations\n\n1. **Debounce Validation**: Don't validate on every keystroke; debounce updates:\n\n```typescript\nlet timeout: NodeJS.Timeout;\nvscode.workspace.onDidChangeTextDocument(event => {\n    clearTimeout(timeout);\n    timeout = setTimeout(() => {\n        validateDocument(event.document);\n    }, 500);\n});\n```\n\n2. **Workspace-Wide Operations**: Getting all diagnostics can be expensive:\n\n```typescript\n// Expensive for large workspaces\nconst all = vscode.languages.getDiagnostics();\n\n// Better: Get diagnostics only when needed\nconst uri = vscode.window.activeTextEditor?.document.uri;\nif (uri) {\n    const diags = vscode.languages.getDiagnostics(uri);\n}\n```\n\n3. **Reuse Collections**: Don't create new collections frequently:\n\n```typescript\n// Bad: New collection on each activation\nexport function activate() {\n    const collection = createDiagnosticCollection();  // Every time\n}\n\n// Good: Create once, reuse\nconst collection = vscode.languages.createDiagnosticCollection('myExt');\nexport function activate() {\n    // Use existing collection\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Threading and Async Behavior\n\n- **No threading issues**: All Diagnostic API operations are synchronous and occur on the main thread.\n- **Async validation**: If your validation logic is async, store results and call `set()` when ready:\n\n```typescript\nvscode.workspace.onDidChangeTextDocument(async event => {\n    const diagnostics = await performAsyncValidation(event.document);\n    collection.set(event.document.uri, diagnostics);  // Sync API call\n});\n```\n\n---\n\n## Summary\n\nThe Diagnostic API provides a robust system for reporting code issues:\n\n| API Element | Purpose |\n|-------------|---------|\n| `Diagnostic` | Represents a single issue with range, message, and severity |\n| `DiagnosticCollection` | Manages diagnostics for multiple files |\n| `DiagnosticSeverity` | Categorizes issue importance (Error, Warning, Information, Hint) |\n| `DiagnosticTag` | Adds visual rendering hints (Unnecessary, Deprecated) |\n| `DiagnosticRelatedInformation` | Links related locations for context |\n| `createDiagnosticCollection()` | Factory for new collections |\n| `getDiagnostics()` | Retrieves current diagnostics |\n| `onDidChangeDiagnostics` | Event fired when diagnostics change |\n\nProper use of these APIs allows extensions to provide users with clear, actionable feedback about code issues directly within VS Code.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
